## assistant

The clone is still running (the model is about a gigabyte and it's CPU-only, so give it a few more minutes).

Here's where the design has landed. This is the loop, and it repeats seven times:

| Beat | Sound | Picture |
|---|---|---|
| Reads a Chunk | Interior. Close, dry, quiet, held tone underneath | Camera micro-drifts along the line. Everything else soft |
| Stops reading | Bed drops out | Focus releases, whole page softens |
| Glances off | Room acoustic returns | Monitor slides to frame edge, stays soft |
| Tells it back, Colours In | His real voice, present, in the room | Words surface as he speaks them |
| Looks back | | Check, check, check across what he carried |
| Next Chunk | | Focus re-acquires |

Then, once per stimulus: Space, the page clears, and the cloned voice gives the whole thing back from memory.

**What's settled and won't move without you saying so:** true first person, no face, no hands. Your real recordings for everything except The Recall. Ninety seconds of cloned voice for the part that never existed. Annotations physically present in the scene rather than laid over it, because that's what stops them reading as app features. Real stimulus text, rendered properly, never generated. Natural language on screen, no product vocabulary. Scaffolding fading from Q1 to Q3.

**Two things I'm holding rather than acting on:** the grader change you raised, since that's the app and not this film. And the four design tracks, which I launched before the last few decisions landed and which are now partly out of date. I'll re-run the affected ones against Round 4 rather than hand you stale work.

One question left before I start producing.

<!doctype html>
<!--
  KALLAWAY · tier2 · lower-third · NAMEPLATE3D
  An extruded 3D glass nameplate rendered with CSS perspective tilt — the card
  leans back in space, revealing a thick glowing side-depth edge. The name sits
  on the face; role on a dimmer sub-ledge. Sits over live speaker footage.
  Slots: name (~26 chars), role (~40 chars).
-->
<html>
  <head>
    <meta charset="utf-8" />
    <link rel="stylesheet" href="../../tokens.css" />
    <style>
      html, body { margin: 0; background: transparent; }
      #t2-lt-np3d {
        position: absolute; inset: 0; width: 1920px; height: 1080px;
        background: transparent; overflow: hidden;
        font-family: var(--font-display);
      }
      /* Localized ambient bloom behind the card */
      #t2-lt-np3d .local-bloom {
        position: absolute;
        left: 40px; bottom: 80px;
        width: 600px; height: 260px;
        border-radius: 50%;
        background: radial-gradient(ellipse at 40% 80%,
          rgba(var(--accent-rgb), 0.30) 0%,
          rgba(var(--accent-rgb), 0.10) 48%,
          transparent 75%);
        filter: blur(40px);
        pointer-events: none;
        z-index: 1;
      }
      /* Perspective stage */
      #t2-lt-np3d .stage {
        position: absolute;
        left: 80px; bottom: 130px;
        perspective: 900px;
        perspective-origin: 30% 60%;
        z-index: 2;
      }
      /* The 3D-tilted plate */
      #t2-lt-np3d .plate-3d {
        position: relative;
        width: 500px;
        transform-style: preserve-3d;
        transform: rotateX(10deg) rotateY(-7deg);
      }
      /* Face of the nameplate */
      #t2-lt-np3d .face {

This is what makes \"borrow, don't switch\" actually\nwork, rather than being a compromise.\n\n### 1. His \"check-check-check\" -> `t2-lb-checkmark.html`\nSVG check mark that DRAWS ON via `stroke-dashoffset`, with an SVG glow filter,\nfollowed by a term phrase and a detail line. Transparent overlay, paints over\nlive footage. Exactly the verification beat he wants when the eye returns to the\nscreen.\n\n### 2. His \"bubbles, words coming out as he speaks\" -> `t2-lb-bubble.html`\nGlass speech bubble with a pointer tail, scaling up from zero on a `back.out`\nspring. This is his \"not just like a caption but bubbles\" note, already built.\n\n### 3. His \"3D, physically real, not cartoon, not static\" -> `t2-lt-nameplate3d.html`\nThis is the important one. The technique:\n  perspective: 900px;  perspective-origin: 30% 60%;\n  transform-style: preserve-3d;\n  transform: rotateX(10deg) rotateY(-7deg);\nplus an extruded side-depth edge and a **localized radial bloom behind the\ncomponent** (blur(40px), accent colour, its own z-index) rather than a global\nglow. ## assistant

Three cards in that library map onto three things you asked for by name, which is worth more than the fourteen skills.

**"Check-check-check"** → `t2-lb-checkmark.html`. An SVG check that draws itself on via `stroke-dashoffset` with a glow filter, painting over live footage. That's your verification beat, already built.

**"Bubbles, words coming out as he speaks"** → `t2-lb-bubble.html`. A glass speech bubble with a pointer tail that springs up from zero. Your exact note, already built.

**"3D, physically real, ingrained in the video, not cartoon"** → `t2-lt-nameplate3d.html`, and this is the valuable one. The technique is `perspective: 900px` with `preserve-3d` and a slight `rotateX/rotateY` lean, an extruded side edge so the card has real thickness, and critically a **localized bloom behind each component** rather than a global glow. That last part is the trick. A local bloom makes an overlay look *lit by the scene* instead of pasted onto it. Match that perspective to the Kling plate's and the annotation stops being an overlay and becomes an object in the room.

**The structural point that makes all of this free:** every card is plain HTML, CSS, SVG and GSAP. Remotion renders HTML. So these lift straight into the project you already have, verified at 18 of 18, and we never touch the HyperFrames renderer. Borrowing was the right call and now it costs nothing.

Still rejecting `MOTION_PHILOSOPHY`'s pacing rules. Its craft is excellent and I'm taking it. Its grammar is built for a thirty-second advert: 1.5-second cuts, 90 percent black frames, motion blur on every transition. Applied to a film whose hero object is a paragraph someone must read, it would produce something beautiful that teaches nothing.

The voice clone is still grinding on CPU. When it lands I'll send you your own voice reading the Q1 Recall so you can hear whether it holds up, and then I start building Q1.

Your real voice on top, the clone underneath, both playable in the page.

The voice problem is solved, and better than the paths we were considering. **Chatterbox, MIT licensed, running locally on your machine, zero credits, commercial use covered.** No ElevenLabs subscription, no Higgsfield top-up, nothing leaving the box.

Two things I hit and fixed, both recorded so they don't cost anyone an hour later. Installing Chatterbox downgrades numpy and breaks Kokoro, so those two can't share an environment — moot now, since cloning you beats picking a preset. And `perth`, its watermarking dependency, imports as null on this machine and kills model loading with a misleading error. Stubbed it, which is fine given it's your voice in your own product.

**What to actually listen for:** not whether it sounds robotic, because it won't. Whether it sounds like *you*. In the film it lands immediately after ninety seconds of your real recording, so the ear gets a direct comparison. Rhythm gives clones away more often than timbre.

If it's close but off, there are dials worth pulling before we give up on it: more than 21.9 seconds of reference, a different reference passage, or the pacing settings.

**Where the whole thing stands:** the design is settled through Round 4, the loop grammar is written down beat by beat, Kling is logged in with 660 credits and confirmed 4K, the motion techniques for your check marks, speech bubbles and physically-real annotations are identified in the kit, and now the voice works. import React from "react";
import { Composition } from "remotion";
import { RRC, plan, type Payload } from "./rrc/RRC";

import stimuli from "../public/content/stimuli.json";
import timeline from "../public/content/timeline.json";
import c1 from "../public/content/PT105_S2_Q1.cues.json";
import c2 from "../public/content/PT105_S2_Q2.cues.json";
import c3 from "../public/content/PT105_S2_Q3.cues.json";

const FPS = 30;
const payload = {
  stimuli: stimuli as Payload["stimuli"],
  timeline: timeline as Payload["timeline"],
  cues: { PT105_S2_Q1: c1, PT105_S2_Q2: c2, PT105_S2_Q3: c3 } as Payload["cues"],
};

export const RemotionRoot: React.FC = () => (
  <Composition
    id="RRCWalkthrough"
    component={RRC as never}
    durationInFrames={plan(payload.timeline, FPS).total}
    fps={FPS}
    width={1920}
    height={1080}
    defaultProps={payload as never}
  />
);

=== RRC.tsx ===
import React from "react";
import { AbsoluteFill, Sequence, useVideoConfig } from "remotion";
import { loadFont as loadFraunces } from "@remotion/google-fonts/Fraunces";
import { TitleCard, ReadyBeat, Working, RecallCard, Outro, type Seg, type Cue } from "./Scenes";
import type { Chunk } from "./Stimulus";
import { SEC } from "./tokens";

loadFraunces("normal", {
  weights: ["400"],
  subsets: ["latin"],
  ignoreTooManyRequestsWarning: true,
});

export type Payload = {
  stimuli: Record<string, { slug: string; chunks: Chunk[] }>;
  timeline: Record<string, { duration: number; segments: Seg[] }>;
  cues: Record<string, Cue[]>;
};

export const ORDER = ["PT105_S2_Q1", "PT105_S2_Q2", "PT105_S2_Q3"];
export const TITLE_S = 9, READY_S = 4, RECALL_S = 9, OUTRO_S = 9;

/** Frame-exact plan, so duration is derived once and never guessed twice. */
export function plan(timeline: Payload["timeline"], fps: number) {
  const out: { name: string; from: number; frames: number; slug?: string }[] = [];
  let f = 0;
  const push = (name: string, secs: number, slug?: string) => {
    const frames = Math.round(secs * fps);
    out.push({ name, from: f, frames, slug }); f += frames;
  };
  push("title", TITLE_S);
  push("ready", READY_S);
  for (const slug of ORDER) {
    push(`work:${slug}`, timeline[slug].duration, slug);
    push(`recall:${slug}`, RECALL_S, slug);
  }
  push("outro", OUTRO_S);
  return { scenes: out, total: f };
}

export const RRC: React.FC<Payload> = ({ stimuli, timeline, cues }) => {
  const { fps } = useVideoConfig();
  const { scenes } = plan(timeline, fps);
  return (
    <AbsoluteFill style={{ background: SEC.field }}>
      {scenes.map((s) => {
        const i = s.slug ? ORDER.indexOf(s.slug) : -1;
        let inner: React.ReactNode = null;
        if (s.name === "title") inner = <TitleCard />;
        else if (s.name === "ready") inner = <ReadyBeat />;
        else if (s.name.startsWith("work:") && s.slug)
          inner = (
            <Working
              slug={s.slug}
              chunks={stimuli[s.slug].chunks}
              segments={timeline[s.slug].segments}
              cues={cues[s.slug]}
              where="PT105 Section 2"
              qIndex={i + 1}
              qTotal={23}
            />
          );
        else if (s.name.startsWith("recall:") && s.slug)
          inner = <RecallCard detailCount={stimuli[s.slug].chunks.reduce((a, c) => a + c.details.length, 0)} />;
        else if (s.name === "outro") inner = <Outro />;
        return (
          <Sequence key={s.name} from={s.from} durationInFrames={s.frames} name={s.name}>
            {inner}
          </Sequence>
        );
      })}
    </AbsoluteFill>
  );
};

=== Screen.tsx ===
import React from "react";
import { SEC, LOGICAL_W, LOGICAL_H } from "./tokens";

/* A faithful replica of `SectionStage`: indigo field, white header rail, one
   centred white paper card. Presentational only, exactly as the real one is. */
export const Screen: React.FC<{
  children: React.ReactNode;
  through: number;      // progress 0..1
  clock: string;
  where: string;
  instruction: string;
  keyDoes: string;
}> = ({ children, through, clock, where, instruction, keyDoes }) => (
  <div
    style={{
      width: LOGICAL_W, height: LOGICAL_H, background: SEC.field,
      fontFamily: SEC.sans, display: "flex", flexDirection: "column",
    }}
  >
    {/* header rail */}
    <header
      style={{
        display: "flex", alignItems: "center", gap: 16,
        padding: "0.75rem 1.5rem", borderBottom: `1px solid ${SEC.hairline}`,
        background: SEC.paper, color: SEC.chrome, fontSize: 13,
      }}
    >
      <span style={{ fontWeight: 500 }}>{where}</span>
      <div style={{ flex: 1, height: 3, background: SEC.hairline, borderRadius: 2, overflow: "hidden" }}>
        <div style={{ height: "100%", width: "100%", background: SEC.chrome,
                      transform: `scaleX(${through})`, transformOrigin: "left" }} />
      </div>
      <span style={{ fontVariantNumeric: "tabular-nums" }}>{clock}</span>
      <span style={{ display: "flex", alignItems: "center", gap: 6, color: "#b91c1c" }}>
        <i style={{ width: 8, height: 8, borderRadius: 99, background: "#b91c1c", display: "block" }} />
        REC
      </span>
    </header>

    {/* field + paper */}
    <main style={{ flex: 1, display: "flex", padding: "1rem", minHeight: 0 }}>
      <div
        style={{
          display: "flex", flexDirection: "column", width: "100%", maxWidth: 1400,
          margin: "0 auto", border: `1px solid ${SEC.rule}`, borderRadius: 6,
          background: SEC.paper, overflow: "hidden",
          boxShadow: "0 4px 18px rgb(0 0 0 / 0.35)",
        }}
      >
        <div style={{ flex: 1, display: "flex", justifyContent: "center", padding: "2rem 1.5rem", minHeight: 0 }}>
          {children}
        </div>
        <div
          style={{
            display: "flex", alignItems: "center", justifyContent: "space-between",
            padding: "0.875rem 1.5rem", borderTop: `1px solid ${SEC.hairline}`,
            background: SEC.foot, fontSize: 13, color: SEC.chrome,
          }}
        >
          <span style={{ color: SEC.ink, fontWeight: 500 }}>{instruction}</span>
          <span>
            <kbd style={{ padding: "2px 8px", border: `1px solid ${SEC.rule}`, borderRadius: 4,
                          background: SEC.paper, fontFamily: SEC.sans }}>Space</kbd>
            <span style={{ marginLeft: 8 }}>{keyDoes}</span>
          </span>
        </div>
      </div>
    </main>
  </div>
);

mn = a.min(axis=2)\n    mask = (mn > 150) & ((mx-mn) < 26)\n    # largest connected component via simple label using scipy-free flood on downsample\n    ys,xs = np.nonzero(mask)\n    if len(xs)==0:\n        print(name,\"no mask\"); n=0\n                while q:\n                    y,x=q.popleft(); n+=1\n                    for dy,dx in ((1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)):\n                        yy,xx=y+dy,x+dx\n                        if 0<=yy<small.shape[0] and 0<=xx<small.shape[1] and small[yy,xx] and lab[yy,xx]==0:\n                            lab[yy,xx]=cur; import React from "react";
import { SEC, TEACH } from "./tokens";

export type Chunk = { n: number; text: string; details: { n: number; text: string }[] };

/* The stimulus exactly as the drill renders it: every chunk in order, nothing
   marking one out from another. The teaching highlight is layered ON TOP and is
   the only thing here the real screen does not do. */
export const Stimulus: React.FC<{
  chunks: Chunk[];
  activeNs: number[];
  reading: boolean;
}> = ({ chunks, activeNs, reading }) => (
  <div
    style={{
      maxWidth: "52ch", color: SEC.ink, fontFamily: SEC.serif,
      fontSize: SEC.stimulusPx, fontWeight: 400, lineHeight: SEC.stimulusLh,
      fontOpticalSizing: "none", fontVariationSettings: '"opsz" 14',
    }}
  >
    {chunks.map((c) => {
      const on = activeNs.includes(c.n);
      return (
        <span
          key={c.n}
          style={{
            background: on ? TEACH.amberSoft : "transparent",
            boxShadow: on ? `inset 0 -2px 0 ${TEACH.amber}` : "none",
            borderRadius: 2,
            transition: "background 200ms",
          }}
        >
          {c.text}{" "}
        </span>
      );
    })}
  </div>
);

export const Captions: React.FC<{ text: string }> = ({ text }) =>
  text ? (
    <div style={{
      position: "absolute", bottom: 150, left: "50%", transform: "translateX(-50%)",
      maxWidth: 1180, padding: "14px 26px", borderRadius: 10,
      background: "rgba(8,6,20,0.9)", color: "#f8fafc",
      fontFamily: SEC.sans, fontSize: 30, lineHeight: 1.34, textAlign: "center",
    }}>
      {text}
    </div>
  ) : null;

/* Names the step she is in. The label a student reads for step 2 is always
   "Tell it back" (CONTEXT.md glossary), never "Say it back". */
export const StepRail: React.FC<{ step: string; note: string }> = ({ step, note }) => (

    position: "absolute", left: 32, top: 28, zIndex: 5, width: 300,
    padding: "18px 22px", borderRadius: 12,

    <div style={{ color: TEACH.ink, fontSize: 19, marginTop: 10, lineHeight: 1.45 }}>{note}</div>
  </div>
);

import React from "react";
import { SEC, TEACH, LOGICAL_W, LOGICAL_H } from "./tokens";

/* Layout law for this video: the teaching layer gets its own 96px band across
   the top and NEVER overlaps the replicated app. The first cut floated the rail
   and the badge over the app's header rail and clipped it, which is exactly the
   confusion the Q1 decision forbids. */
export const BAND_H = 96;
const APP_H = 1080 - BAND_H;
export const APP_SCALE = APP_H / LOGICAL_H;          // 984 / 720
const APP_W = LOGICAL_W * APP_SCALE;
export const APP_X = (1920 - APP_W) / 2;

export const TeachBand: React.FC<{ step: string; note: string }> = ({ step, note }) => (
  <div style={{
    position: "absolute", top: 0, left: 0, width: 1920, height: BAND_H, zIndex: 6,
    display: "flex", alignItems: "center", gap: 20, padding: "0 34px",
    background: "#0d0a1f", borderBottom: `2px solid ${TEACH.amber}`,
    fontFamily: SEC.sans, boxSizing: "border-box",
  }}>
    <span style={{ color: TEACH.amber, fontSize: 14, letterSpacing: 1.6,
                   textTransform: "uppercase", whiteSpace: "nowrap" }}>
      What he is doing
    </span>
    <span style={{ color: "#fff", fontSize: 30, fontWeight: 500, whiteSpace: "nowrap" }}>{step}</span>
    <span style={{ color: TEACH.ink, fontSize: 20, opacity: 0.92 }}>{note}</span>
    <span style={{ marginLeft: "auto", display: "flex", alignItems: "center", gap: 9,
                   padding: "8px 16px", borderRadius: 999, border: `1.5px solid ${TEACH.amber}`,
                   color: TEACH.ink, fontSize: 16, whiteSpace: "nowrap" }}>
      <span style={{ width: 8, height: 8, borderRadius: 99, background: TEACH.amber }} />
      Teaching overlay, not part of the drill
    </span>
  </div>
);

/** Positions the replicated app under the band, never behind it. */
export const AppFrame: React.FC<{ children: React.ReactNode }> = ({ children }) => (
  <div style={{
    position: "absolute", top: BAND_H, left: APP_X,
    width: LOGICAL_W, height: LOGICAL_H,
    transform: `scale(${APP_SCALE})`, transformOrigin: "top left",
  }}>
    {children}
  </div>
);

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\video\\src\\pov\\quad.ts", "content": "/*\n * Mapping the replicated app screen onto the monitor in the generated plate.\n *\n * The quad below was DETECTED from public/plates/room-v3.png, not eyeballed:\n * the screen is the largest light, low-saturation region in the image, and its\n * four extreme corners were taken from that mask. Left edge 1936px, right edge\n * 1668px, so the monitor is turned 13.8% away from the camera. Any new plate\n * must be re-detected, never adjusted by hand.\n */\n\nexport type Pt = readonly [number, number];\n\n/** Screen corners in room-v3.png, normalised 0..1. TL, TR, BR, BL. */\nexport const SCREEN_QUAD: readonly [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt] = [\n  [0.0712, 0.1198],\n  [0.6156, 0.1839],\n  [0.6052, 0.7266],\n  [0.0752, 0.7500],\n] as const;\n\n/** The plate is 5504x3072 (1.792:1), slightly wider than the 16:9 frame. */\nexport const PLATE_W = 5504;\nexport const PLATE_H = 3072;\n\n/**\n * CSS matrix3d that maps an axis-aligned w x h element onto an arbitrary quad.\n *\n * Solves the projective transform taking the unit square to (p0,p1,p2,p3),\n * then post-multiplies by scale(1/w, 1/h) so the element's own pixel size is\n * absorbed. Requires transformOrigin \"0 0\" on the element.\n */\nexport function quadTransform(\n  w: number,\n  h: number,\n  p0: Pt, p1: Pt, p2: Pt, p3: Pt,\n): string {\n  const [x0, y0] = p0, [x1, y1] = p1, [x2, y2] = p2, [x3, y3] = p3;\n\n  const dx1 = x1 - x2, dx2 = x3 - x2, sx = x0 - x1 + x2 - x3;\n  const dy1 = y1 - y2, dy2 = y3 - y2, sy = y0 - y1 + y2 - y3;\n\n  const den = dx1 * dy2 - dx2 * dy1;\n  const g = den === 0 ? 0 : (sx * dy2 - dx2 * sy) / den;\n  const hh = den === 0 ? 0 : (dx1 * sy - sx * dy1) / den;\n\n  const a = x1 - x0 + g * x1;\n  const b = x3 - x0 + hh * x3;\n  const c = x0;\n  const d = y1 - y0 + g * y1;\n  const e = y3 - y0 + hh * y3;\n  const f = y0;\n\n  // columns divided by the element's own dimensions\n  const m = [\n    a / w, d / w, 0, g / w,\n    b / h, e / h, 0, hh / h,\n    0, 0, 1, 0,\n    c, f, 0, 1,\n  ];\n  return `matrix3d(${m.map((n) => Number(n.toFixed(6))).join(\",\")})`;\n}\n\n/** The quad in frame pixels, for a frame of the given size (plate covers it). */\nexport function screenQuadPx(frameW: number, frameH: number): [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt] {\n  // The plate is scaled to COVER the frame, then centred.\n  const scale = Math.max(frameW / PLATE_W, frameH / PLATE_H);\n  const drawnW = PLATE_W * scale, drawnH = PLATE_H * scale;\n  const offX = (frameW - drawnW) / 2, offY = (frameH - drawnH) / 2;\n  return SCREEN_QUAD.map(\n    ([nx, ny]) => [offX + nx * drawnW, offY + ny * drawnH] as Pt,\n  ) as [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt];\n}\n"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\video\\src\\pov\\beats.ts", "content": "/*\n * The loop, as data.\n *\n * Every beat below comes from public/content/timeline.json, which was produced\n * by global Needleman-Wunsch alignment of walkthrough_md against the Whisper\n * word stream. Nothing here is authored timing; it is his real run.\n *\n *   readStart -> readEnd    he is reading the Chunk IN HIS HEAD\n *   readEnd   -> saidStart  the release: he stops reading, before he speaks\n *   saidStart -> returnAt   he has glanced off and is speaking out loud\n *   returnAt  -> workEnd    his eye comes back and what he carried is confirmed\n */\n\nexport type Seg = {\n  gi: number;\n  chunkNs: number[];\n  readStart: number;\n  readEnd: number;\n  saidStart: number;\n  workEnd: number;\n  readCoverage: number;\n};\n\nexport type Phase = \"read\" | \"release\" | \"speak\" | \"confirm\";\n\n/** How long the eye-returns-and-confirms beat runs, in seconds. */\nexport const CONFIRM_S = 3.2;\n\nexport function returnAt(s: Seg): number {\n  // Never eat more than a third of the speaking stretch.\n  const speakLen = s.workEnd - s.saidStart;\n  return s.workEnd - Math.min(CONFIRM_S, speakLen / 3);\n}\n\nexport function phaseAt(s: Seg, t: number): Phase {\n  if (t < s.readEnd) return \"read\";\n  if (t < s.saidStart) return \"release\";\n  if (t < returnAt(s)) return \"speak\";\n  return \"confirm\";\n}\n\nexport function segAt(segments: Seg[], t: number): Seg {\n  for (const s of segments) if (t < s.workEnd) return s;\n  return segments[segments.length - 1];\n}\n\n/** 0..1 through the current phase, for driving camera and focus. */\nexport function phaseProgress(s: Seg, t: number): number {\n  const p = phaseAt(s, t);\n  const span =\n    p === \"read\" ? [s.readStart, s.readEnd] :\n    p === \"release\" ? [s.readEnd, s.saidStart] :\n    p === \"speak\" ? [s.saidStart, returnAt(s)] :\n    [returnAt(s), s.workEnd];\n  const [a, b] = span;\n  if (b <= a) return 1;\n  return Math.min(1, Math.max(0, (t - a) / (b - a)));\n}\n"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\video\\src\\pov\\Room.tsx", "content": "import React from \"react\";\nimport { AbsoluteFill, Img, staticFile, interpolate, Easing } from \"remotion\";\nimport { SEC, LOGICAL_W, LOGICAL_H } from \"../rrc/tokens\";\nimport { quadTransform, screenQuadPx } from \"./quad\";\nimport type { Chunk } from \"../rrc/Stimulus\";\nimport type { Seg, Phase } from \"./beats\";\nimport { phaseAt, phaseProgress, returnAt } from \"./beats\";\n\n/*\n * The POV room.\n *\n * Plate, screen and every floating card live inside ONE camera wrapper, so the\n * whole world moves together. That is what makes an annotation read as an\n * object in the room rather than an overlay on the frame.\n *\n * Two rules this file exists to keep:\n *  1. The screen is Remotion, never generated. Kling cannot render the stimulus.\n *  2. Nothing marks the text. Attention is shown with FOCUS, because focus is a\n *     property of the eye and cannot be mistaken for an app feature.\n */\n\n/** How far the eye slides off the text when he looks away, in frame px. */\nconst GLANCE_X = 232;\nconst GLANCE_Y = 46;\n/** Slight push in, so panning never exposes the plate's edge. */\nconst BASE_ZOOM = 1.075;\n\nconst ease = Easing.bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1);\n\nexport function cameraAt(s: Seg, t: number): { x: number; y: number; zoom: number } {\n  const p = phaseAt(s, t);\n  const k = phaseProgress(s, t);\n\n  if (p === \"read\") {\n    // Micro-drift down the Chunk, the way an eye tracks a line. Deliberately\n    // small: this should be felt, not seen.\n    return { x: -6 * k, y: 7 * k, zoom: BASE_ZOOM + 0.006 * k };\n  }\n  if (p === \"release\") {\n    // Focus lets go before the eye does.\n    return { x: interpolate(k, [0, 1], [-6, -26], { easing: ease }),\n             y: interpolate(k, [0, 1], [7, 12], { easing: ease }),\n             zoom: BASE_ZOOM + 0.006 };\n  }\n  if (p === \"speak\") {\n    // Glanced off. Settles quickly, then hangs with a slow living drift.\n    const settle = interpolate(k, [0, 0.14], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: \"clamp\", easing: ease });\n    const drift = Math.sin(k * Math.PI * 1.6) * 9;\n    return { x: -26 - (GLANCE_X - 26) * settle + drift,\n             y: 12 + (GLANCE_Y - 12) * settle + drift * 0.35,\n             zoom: BASE_ZOOM + 0.006 - 0.012 * settle };\n  }\n  // confirm: the eye comes back to the page\n  const back = interpolate(k, [0, 0.42], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: \"clamp\", easing: ease });\n  return { x: interpolate(back, [0, 1], [-GLANCE_X, 0]),\n           y: interpolate(back, [0, 1], [GLANCE_Y, 0]),\n           zoom: interpolate(back, [0, 1], [BASE_ZOOM - 0.006, BASE_ZOOM]) };\n}\n\n/** Blur applied to a Chunk, in px. Zero only for the Chunk being read. */\nexport function chunkBlur(active: boolean, phase: Phase, k: number): number {\n  if (phase === \"read\") return active ? 0 : 2.6;\n  if (phase === \"release\") return active ? interpolate(k, [0, 1], [0, 2.2]) : 2.6;\n  if (phase === \"speak\") return 3.1;              // he is not reading any of it\n  return interpolate(k, [0, 0.42], [3.1, 0.9], { extrapolateRight: \"clamp\" });\n}\n\n/** The stimulus as the drill renders it, with optical defocus and no marking. */\nexport const POVStimulus: React.FC<{\n  chunks: Chunk[];\n  activeNs: number[];\n  phase: Phase;\n  k: number;\n}> = ({ chunks, activeNs, phase, k }) => (\n  <div\n    style={{\n      maxWidth: \"52ch\", color: SEC.ink, fontFamily: SEC.serif,\n      fontSize: SEC.stimulusPx, fontWeight: 400, lineHeight: SEC.stimulusLh,\n      fontOpticalSizing: \"none\", fontVariationSettings: '\"opsz\" 14',\n    }}\n  >\n    {chunks.map((c) => {\n      const on = activeNs.includes(c.n);\n      const b = chunkBlur(on, phase, k);\n      return (\n        <span\n          key={c.n}\n          style={{\n            filter: b > 0.02 ? `blur(${b.toFixed(2)}px)` : \"none\",\n            opacity: b > 0.02 ? 0.62 : 1,\n            display: \"inline\",\n          }}\n        >\n          {c.text}{\" \"}\n        </span>\n      );\n    })}\n  </div>\n);\n\n/** Composites any screen content onto the monitor in the plate. */\nexport const Monitor: React.FC<{\n  frameW: number; frameH: number; children: React.ReactNode;\n}> = ({ frameW, frameH, children }) => {\n  const [p0, p1, p2, p3] = screenQuadPx(frameW, frameH);\n  return (\n    <div\n      style={{\n        position: \"absolute\", top: 0, left: 0,\n        width: LOGICAL_W, height: LOGICAL_H,\n        transform: quadTransform(LOGICAL_W, LOGICAL_H, p0, p1, p2, p3),\n        transformOrigin: \"0 0\",\n        // The panel is emissive; a touch of light spills past its edges.\n        boxShadow: \"0 0 120px 24px rgba(150,170,255,0.10)\",\n      }}\n    >\n      {children}\n    </div>\n  );\n};\n\nexport const Room: React.FC<{\n  seg: Seg; t: number; frameW: number; frameH: number; children: React.ReactNode;\n}> = ({ seg, t, frameW, frameH, children }) => {\n  const cam = cameraAt(seg, t);\n  return (\n    <AbsoluteFill style={{ overflow: \"hidden\", background: \"#0b0a10\" }}>\n      <div\n        style={{\n          position: \"absolute\", inset: 0,\n          transform: `translate(${cam.x.toFixed(2)}px, ${cam.y.toFixed(2)}px) scale(${cam.zoom.toFixed(4)})`,\n          transformOrigin: \"50% 50%\",\n        }}\n      >\n        <Img\n          src={staticFile(\"plates/room-v3.png\")}\n          style={{ position: \"absolute\", inset: 0, width: \"100%\", height: \"100%\", objectFit: \"cover\" }}\n        />\n        {children}\n      </div>\n    </AbsoluteFill>\n  );\n};\n\nexport { returnAt };\n"}

They must never be mistaken\n * for part of the drill, and here that is guaranteed by geometry: nothing in the\n * real drill floats in your room.\n */\n\nconst GLASS =\n  \"linear-gradient(135deg, rgba(255,255,255,.10) 0%, rgba(255,255,255,.035) 38%, \" +\n  \"rgba(255,255,255,.014) 66%, rgba(255,255,255,.075) 100%)\";\n\nconst Bloom: React.FC<{ w: number; h: number; rgb: string; a?: number }> = ({ w, h, rgb, a = 0.3 }) => (\n  <div\n    style={{\n      position: \"absolute\", left: -w * 0.18, top: -h * 0.22,\n      width: w * 1.36, height: h * 1.44, borderRadius: \"50%\",\n      background: `radial-gradient(ellipse at 42% 78%, rgba(${rgb},${a}) 0%, rgba(${rgb},${a * 0.33}) 48%, transparent 74%)`,\n      filter: \"blur(38px)\", pointerEvents: \"none\", zIndex: 0,\n    }}\n  />\n);\n\n/** A card leaning in the room's space. Children sit on its face. */\nexport const Plate3D: React.FC<{\n  x: number; y: number; w: number;\n  tiltX?: number; tiltY?: number;\n  rgb?: string; enter: number;           // 0..1\n  children: const scale = interpolate(enter, [0, 1], [0.9, 1], { easing: Easing.out(Easing.cubic) });\n  return (\n    <div style={{ position: \"absolute\", left: x, top: y, perspective: 900, perspectiveOrigin: \"34% 58%\", zIndex: 4 }}>\n      <div\n        style={{\n          position: \"relative\", width: w, transformStyle: \"preserve-3d\",\n          transform: `rotateX(${tiltX}deg) rotateY(${tiltY}deg) translateY(${lift.toFixed(2)}px) scale(${scale.toFixed(3)})`,\n          opacity: interpolate(enter, [0, 0.35], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: \"clamp\" }),\n        }}\n      >\n        <Bloom w={w} h={130} rgb={rgb} />\n        {/* extruded side depth, so the card has real thickness */}\n        <div\n          style={{\n            position: \"absolute\", inset: 0, borderRadius: 16, zIndex: 1,\n            transform: \"translateZ(-13px)\",\n            background: `rgba(${rgb},0.30)`,\n            filter: \"blur(1px)\",\n          }}\n        />\n        <div\n          style={{\n            position: \"relative\", zIndex: 2, borderRadius: 16,\n            background: GLASS,\n            backdropFilter: \"blur(14px) saturate(1.12)\",\n            WebkitBackdropFilter: \"blur(14px) saturate(1.12)\",\n            border: \"1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.17)\",\n            boxShadow:\n              \"inset 0 1px 0 rgba(255,255,255,0.22), 0 26px 60px rgba(0,0,0,0.46)\",\n            padding: \"20px 24px\",\n            fontFamily: SEC.sans, color: \"#fff\",\n          }}\n        >\n          {children}\n        </div>\n      </div>\n    </div>\n  );\n};\n\n/** What he is saying, arriving as he says it. */\nexport const SpeechBubble: React.FC<{ text: string; enter: number; x: number; y: number }> = ({\n  text, enter, x, y,\n}) => (\n  <Plate3D x={x} y={y} w={620} tiltX={6} tiltY={-11} rgb=\"150,170,255\" enter={enter}>\n    <div style={{ fontSize: 33, lineHeight: 1.36, fontWeight: 400, letterSpacing: -0.2 }}>{text}</div>\n    {/* pointer tail, back toward him */}\n    <div\n      style={{\n        position: \"absolute\", left: 44, bottom: -13, width: 30, height: 30,\n        background: \"rgba(255,255,255,0.075)\",\n        borderRight: \"1px solid x=\"-40%\" y=\"-40%\" width=\"180%\" height=\"180%\">\n            <feGaussianBlur stdDeviation=\"2.4\" result=\"b\" />\n            <feMerge><feMergeNode in=\"b\" /><feMergeNode in=\"SourceGraphic\" /></feMerge>\n          </filter>\n        </defs>\n        <path\n          d=\"M6 19 L14.5 27 L30 9\"\n          stroke=\"#7ee0b0\" strokeWidth={4.2} strokeLinecap=\"round\" {"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\video\\src\\pov\\POVWorking.tsx", "content": "import React from \"react\";\nimport { AbsoluteFill, Audio, staticFile, useCurrentFrame, useVideoConfig } from \"remotion\";\nimport { Screen } from \"../rrc/Screen\";\nimport type { Chunk } from \"../rrc/Stimulus\";\nimport { Room, Monitor, POVStimulus } from \"./Room\";\nimport { SpeechBubble, CheckLine, Plate3D, useEnter } from \"./Float\";\nimport { segAt, phaseAt, phaseProgress, returnAt, type Seg } from \"./beats\";\n\nexport type Cue = { start: number; end: number; text: string };\n\nconst mmss = (t: number) =>\n  `${Math.floor(t / 60)}:${String(Math.floor(t % 60)).padStart(2, \"0\")}`;\n\n/*\n * One stimulus, in the room.\n *\n * The loop, seven times across the film:\n *   reads a Chunk in his head   camera tracks the line, everything else soft\n *   stops                        focus releases, the whole page softens\n *   glances off                  monitor slides to the frame edge\n *   tells it back, colours in    what he says arrives beside him\n *   looks back                   what he carried is confirmed against the page\n */\nexport const POVWorking: React.FC<{\n  slug: string;\n  chunks: Chunk[];\n  segments: Seg[];\n  cues: Cue[];\n  audioSrc: string;\n  where: string;\n  qIndex: number;\n  qTotal: number;\n}> = ({ slug, chunks, segments, cues, audioSrc, where, qIndex, qTotal }) => {\n  const frame = useCurrentFrame();\n  const { fps, width, height, durationInFrames } = useVideoConfig();\n  const t = frame / fps;\n\n  const seg = segAt(segments, t);\n  const phase = phaseAt(seg, t);\n  const k = phaseProgress(seg, t);\n  const through = Math.min(1, frame / durationInFrames);\n\n  // What he is saying right now, and when it started, so the bubble springs in.\n  const cueIdx = cues.findIndex((c) => t >= c.start && t < c.end);\n  const cue = cueIdx >= 0 ? cues[cueIdx] : null;\n  const cueEnter = useEnter(cue ? Math.round((t - cue.start) * fps) : -1);\n  const showBubble = (phase === \"speak\" || phase === \"release\") && !!cue;\n\n  // On the way back to the page, what he carried is confirmed one line at a time.\n  const groupChunks = chunks.filter((c) => seg.chunkNs.includes(c.n));\n  const carried = groupChunks.flatMap((c) => c.details).slice(0, 5);\n  const confirmFrom = returnAt(seg);\n  const confirmFrames = Math.round((t - confirmFrom) * fps);\n\n  return (\n    <AbsoluteFill>\n      <Audio src={staticFile(audioSrc)} />\n\n      <Room seg={seg} t={t} frameW={width} frameH={height}>\n        <Monitor frameW={width} frameH={height}>\n          <Screen\n            through={through}\n            clock={mmss(t)}\n            where={`${where}  ·  ${qIndex} of ${qTotal}`}\n            instruction=\"Read it. Tell it back and colour it in as you go.\"\n            keyDoes=\"recall full stimulus\"\n          >\n            <POVStimulus chunks={chunks} activeNs={seg.chunkNs} phase={phase} k={k} />\n          </Screen>\n        </Monitor>\n\n        {/* What he is saying, arriving beside him while he is looking away. */}\n        {showBubble && cue && (\n          <SpeechBubble key={cueIdx} text={cue.text} enter={cueEnter} x={1268} y={392} />\n        )}\n\n        {/* Back on the page: confirmation of what he carried. */}\n        {phase === \"confirm\" && carried.length > 0 && (\n          <Plate3D x={1206} y={286} w={600} tiltX={6} tiltY={-10} rgb=\"126,224,176\" enter={useEnter(confirmFrames)}>\n            <div\n              style={{\n                fontSize: 17, letterSpacing: 1.7, textTransform: \"uppercase\",\n                color: \"rgba(126,224,176,0.92)\", marginBottom: 15,\n              }}\n            >\n              He carried\n            </div>\n            <div style={{ display: \"flex\", flexDirection: \"column\", gap: 12 }}>\n              {carried.map((d, i) => (\n                <CheckLine\n                  key={d.n}\n                  text={d.text}\n                  enter={useEnter(confirmFrames - Math.round(0.34 * fps) * (i + 1), 128)}\n                />\n              ))}\n            </div>\n          </Plate3D>\n        )}\n      </Room>\n    </AbsoluteFill>\n  );\n};\n"}

{"replace_all": false, "file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\video\\src\\pov\\quad.ts", "old_string": "/** Screen corners in room-v3.png, normalised 0..1. TL, TR, BR, BL. */\nexport const SCREEN_QUAD: readonly [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt] = [\n  [0.0712, 0.1198],\n  [0.6156, 0.1839],\n  [0.6052, 0.7266],\n  [0.0752, 0.7500],\n] as const;", "new_string": "/**\n * Screen corners in room-v3.png, normalised 0..1. TL, TR, BR, BL.\n *\n * Pushed out from the detected centroid by OVERSCAN. The detector finds the\n * light region, which stops a pixel or two inside the bezel, and the first\n * render showed a grey sliver of the original plate down the right edge. The\n * composite must overshoot into the bezel, never fall short of it.\n */\nconst DETECTED: readonly [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt] = [\n  [0.0712, 0.1198],\n  [0.6156, 0.1839],\n  [0.6052, 0.7266],\n  [0.0752, 0.7500],\n] as const;\n\nconst OVERSCAN = 1.012;\n\nexport const SCREEN_QUAD: readonly [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt] = (() => {\n  const cx = DETECTED.reduce((a, p) => a + p[0], 0) / 4;\n  const cy = DETECTED.reduce((a, p) => a + p[1], 0) / 4;\n  return DETECTED.map(\n    ([x, y]) => [cx + (x - cx) * OVERSCAN, cy + (y - cy) * OVERSCAN] as Pt,\n  ) as unknown as readonly [Pt, Pt, Pt, Pt];\n})();"}

{"command": "cd /c/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/video/public/plates\n/c/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os/projects/hyperframes-student-kit/.venv/Scripts/python.exe - <<'PY'\nimport numpy as np\nfrom PIL import Image\nfrom collections import deque\n\nim = Image.open(\"room-v3.png\").convert(\"RGB\"); W,H = im.size\na = np.asarray(im).astype(np.int16)\nmx = a.max(axis=2); mn = a.min(axis=2)\nmask = (mn > 140) & ((mx-mn) < 34)\n\n# isolate the monitor blob (largest component, downsampled flood)\nsmall = mask[::8,::8]; lab=np.zeros(small.shape,np.int32); cur=0; best=(0,None)\nfor i in range(small.shape[0]):\n    for j in range(small.shape[1]):\n        if small[i,j] and lab[i,j]==0:\n            cur+=1; q=deque([(i,j)]); lab[i,j]=cur; n=0\n            while q:\n                y,x=q.popleft(); n+=1\n                for dy,dx in ((1,0),(-1,0),(0,1),(0,-1)):\n                    yy,xx=y+dy,x+dx\n                    if 0<=yy<small.shape[0] and 0<=xx<small.shape[1] and small[yy,xx] and lab[yy,xx]==0:\n                        lab[yy,xx]=cur; q.append((yy,xx))\n            if n>best[0]: best=(n,cur)\ncomp8 = (lab==best[1])\nys,xs = np.nonzero(comp8)\nY0,Y1,X0,X1 = ys.min()*8, ys.max()*8+8, xs.min()*8, xs.max()*8+8\nsub = mask[Y0:Y1, X0:X1]\nh,w = sub.shape\nprint(\"blob bbox\", X0,Y0,X1,Y1, \"->\", w,\"x\",h)\n\n# per-row extremes, ignoring the top/bottom 6% where corners round off\nrows=[]\nfor r in range(int(h*0.06), int(h*0.94)):\n    xs_=np.nonzero(sub[r])[0]\n    if len(xs_)>w*0.35: rows.append((r, xs_.min(), xs_.max()))\nR=np.array(rows,float)\ndef fit(col):\n    A=np.stack([R[:,0], np.ones(len(R))],1)\n    m,c=np.linalg.lstsq(A, R[:,col], rcond=None)[0]\n    return m,c\nml,cl = fit(1); mr,cr = fit(2)\n\ncols=[]\nfor c_ in range(int(w*0.06), int(w*0.94)):\n    ys_=np.nonzero(sub[:,c_])[0]\n    if len(ys_)>h*0.35: cols.append((c_, ys_.min(), ys_.max()))\nC=np.array(cols,float)\ndef fitc(col):\n    A=np.stack([C[:,0], np.ones(len(C))],1)\n    m,c=np.linalg.lstsq(A, C[:,col], rcond=None)[0]\n    return m,c\nmt,ct = fitc(1); mb,cb = fitc(2)\n\ndef inter(m1,c1,m2,c2,vertical_first=True):\n    # left/right lines: x = m*y + c ; top/bottom: y = m*x + c\n    # solve x = m1*y + c1 and y = m2*x + c2\n    y = (m2*c1 + c2)/(1 - m2*m1); x = m1*y + c1\n    return x,y\n\nTL = inter(ml,cl,mt,ct); TR = inter(mr,cr,mt,ct)\nBR = inter(mr,cr,mb,cb); BL = inter(ml,cl,mb,cb)\nprint(\"\\nfitted corners (normalised to full image):\")\nout=[]\nfor lbl,(x,y) in [(\"TL\",TL),(\"TR\",TR),(\"BR\",BR),(\"BL\",BL)]:\n    gx,gy = (x+X0)/W, (y+Y0)/H\n    out.append((gx,gy)); print(f\"  {lbl}  [{gx:.4f}, {gy:.4f}],\")\nlh=np.hypot(out[3][0]-out[0][0], out[3][1]-out[0][1])\nrh=np.hypot(out[2][0]-out[1][0], out[2][1]-out[1][1])\nprint(f\"  left/right edge ratio {min(lh,rh)/max(lh,rh):.3f}\")\nPY", "description": "Re-detect quad by line fitting", "timeout": 300000}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\video\\align\\treat_audio.py", "content": "\"\"\"Two-layer audio: interior monologue vs spoken in the room.\n\nThe verbatim Chunk reads become interior monologue - closer, drier, darker,\nquieter, the way your own reading voice sounds inside your skull. Everything\nelse stays as recorded, present and in the room. A held tone sits under the\ninterior passages only and drops out the instant he speaks aloud.\n\nDeliberately NOT a heartbeat and NOT a rising pulse: this drill is about calm\nprocessing and a pulse under a timed test reads as anxiety.\n\nSpans come from public/content/timeline.json, which is his real run aligned to\nthe Whisper word stream. Nothing here is authored timing.\n\nTotal duration is preserved exactly, because the picture is cut to it.\n\"\"\"\nimport json, os, subprocess, sys, io\n\nBASE = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))\nTL = json.load(io.open(os.path.join(BASE, \"public/content/timeline.json\"), encoding=\"utf-8\"))\nOUT = os.path.join(BASE, \"public/audio\")\nTMP = os.path.join(BASE, \"align\", \"_tmp\")\nos.makedirs(TMP, exist_ok=True)\n\n# interior: roll off the top so it sits behind the eyes, lift the low-mid a\n# touch for skull resonance, and pull it back in level.\nINTERIOR = (\n    \"highpass=f=95,\"\n    \"lowpass=f=2350,\"\n    \"equalizer=f=220:t=q:w=1.1:g=3.2,\"\n    \"equalizer=f=3000:t=q:w=1.0:g=-4,\"\n    \"volume=0.72\"\n)\n# aloud: barely touched. A hint of early reflection so it belongs to the room.\nALOUD = \"aecho=0.86:0.9:22:0.10,volume=1.0\"\n\ndef run(cmd):\n    r = subprocess.run(cmd, shell=True, capture_output=True, text=True)\n    if r.returncode != 0:\n        print(r.stderr[-1500:]); sys.exit(f\"FAILED: {cmd[:120]}\")\n\ndef spans_for(slug):\n    \"\"\"Ordered (start, end, kind) covering the whole file with no gaps.\"\"\"\n    segs = TL[slug][\"segments\"]\n    dur = TL[slug][\"duration\"]\n    reads = [(s[\"readStart\"], s[\"readEnd\"]) for s in segs]\n    out, cursor = [], 0.0\n    for a, b in reads:\n        if a > cursor:\n            out.append((cursor, a, \"aloud\"))\n        out.append((max(a, cursor), b, \"interior\"))\n        cursor = b\n    if cursor < dur:\n        out.append((cursor, dur, \"aloud\"))\n    return [(a, b, k) for a, b, k in out if b - a > 0.02]\n\ndef treat(slug):\n    src = os.path.join(OUT, f\"{slug}.mp3\")\n    spans = spans_for(slug)\n    parts, listfile = [], os.path.join(TMP, f\"{slug}.txt\")\n    for i, (a, b, kind) in enumerate(spans):\n        p = os.path.join(TMP, f\"{slug}_{i:02d}.wav\")\n        f = INTERIOR if kind == \"interior\" else ALOUD\n        run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -ss {a:.3f} -to {b:.3f} -i \"{src}\" '\n            f'-af \"{f}\" -ar 48000 -ac 1 \"{p}\"')\n        parts.append((p, a, b, kind))\n    with io.open(listfile, \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as fh:\n        for p, *_ in parts:\n            fh.write(f\"file '{os.path.basename(p)}'\\n\")\n    voice = os.path.join(TMP, f\"{slug}_voice.wav\")\n    run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -f concat -safe 0 -i \"{listfile}\" -c copy \"{voice}\"')\n\n    # The bed: two held low tones, under the interior passages only, faded so\n    # neither entry nor exit is ever heard as an event.\n    dur = TL[slug][\"duration\"]\n    gate = []\n    for _, a, b, kind in parts:\n        if kind == \"interior\" and b - a > 0.6:\n            fi, fo = 0.45, 0.40\n            gate.append(f\"between(t,{a:.3f},{b:.3f})*\"\n                        f\"min(1,min((t-{a:.3f})/{fi},({b:.3f}-t)/{fo}))\")\n    env = \"+\".join(gate) if gate else \"0\"\n    bed = os.path.join(TMP, f\"{slug}_bed.wav\")\n    run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -f lavfi -i \"sine=frequency=98:duration={dur:.3f}:sample_rate=48000\" '\n        f'-f lavfi -i \"sine=frequency=147:duration={dur:.3f}:sample_rate=48000\" '\n        f'-filter_complex \"[0:a]volume=0.055[a];[1:a]volume=0.022[b];[a][b]amix=inputs=2:normalize=0,'\n        f'lowpass=f=420,volume=\\'{env}\\':eval=frame[o]\" -map \"[o]\" -ar 48000 -ac 1 \"{bed}\"')\n\n    final = os.path.join(OUT, f\"{slug}.pov.mp3\")\n    run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -i \"{voice}\" -i \"{bed}\" '\n        f'-filter_complex \"[0:a][1:a]amix=inputs=2:normalize=0:duration=first[o]\" '\n        f'-map \"[o]\" -codec:a libmp3lame -b:a 192k \"{final}\"')\n\n    got = subprocess.run(\n        f'ffprobe -v error -show_entries format=duration -of csv=p=0 \"{final}\"',\n        shell=True, capture_output=True, text=True).stdout.strip()\n    n_int = sum(1 for *_, k in parts if k == \"interior\")\n    print(f\"{slug}: {len(parts)} spans ({n_int} interior) | \"\n          f\"expected {dur:.2f}s, got {float(got):.2f}s \"\n          f\"{'OK' if abs(float(got)-dur) < 0.12 else '*** DRIFT ***'}\")\n\nfor slug in (sys.argv[1:] or [\"PT105_S2_Q1\"]):\n    treat(slug)\n"}

## assistant

That's Q1. Ninety-two seconds, 1920x1080, your real audio, three full loops.

**What's in it.** He reads chunk one in his head while the camera tracks the line and the rest of the page sits out of focus. Focus releases, his eye glances off, the monitor slides to the edge of frame, and what he says arrives beside him on a glass card with real depth and its own bloom. Then his eye comes back, the page sharpens, and what he carried ticks off against it. Three times, on the real timings from his run.

**Two defects the render caught that no amount of planning would have.**

The composite was undersized. A grey wedge of the original plate showed down the right edge, widening toward the bottom, because the screen vignettes at its border so the detector stops short of the bezel. I re-detected by line-fitting, and the second method disagreed with the first about the bottom edge, which told me both were undershooting rather than one being wrong. Fixed by overscanning the quad so its edge buries under the bezel, then verified by cropping that edge at high magnification.

The camera pan exposed the plate edges as black bars. The glance is 232 pixels and the wrapper scales about its centre, so the zoom has to clear 1.25 for the translate to stay inside the image. It was at 1.075. Raised to 1.30, which costs nothing since the plate is 5504 pixels wide feeding a 1920 pixel frame.

**One thing I did not fix:** a very thin light line still shows at the screen's right edge at some camera positions. It's much smaller than it was and I'd rather you see it and tell me whether it bothers you than have me spend another render cycle on something you might not notice.

**Also done while that rendered:** all three Recall passages are generated in your cloned voice — 23.5, 29.4 and 22.4 seconds — with per-sentence timing maps. They aren't cut into the film yet, because the Recall beat needs its own picture design: Space pressed, page clears, and the whole argument given back to a blank screen.

Watch it and tell me what's wrong with it.

Building both with the scaffolding fade we decided: Q1 full, Q2 less, Q3 almost none.

One judgement call I'm making explicit. The speech bubbles stay in all three, because they're doing double duty as captions and Round 2 chose burned-in captions throughout on accessibility grounds — that journey scored 2 out of 5 for accessibility in your audit. What fades is the *teaching* furniture: the confirm card and its label.

Now the grilling. Six changes, and one of them decides the film's architecture, so that's first.

Pausing to introduce a step means the audio has to freeze while the instruction plays. The film stops being a continuous playback of your run and becomes your run with teaching cut into it. That's buildable and it's clearly what you're asking for. The question is how often it stops, because Q1 alone has three steps across three chunk groups, and pausing every time is nine stops in ninety seconds.

His voice is muted across every read span, and the\n         span keeps its real duration so the reading PACE stays visible.\n  R5-Q1  each step is introduced with a full stop the first time it appears,\n         and only labelled thereafter.\n  R5-Q3  the labels are Reading / Tell it back / Colouring In, the app's own\n         student-facing words.\n  R5-Q5  The Recall is the real drill exactly: Space, text gone, say it back.\n\nOutput per stimulus:\n  public/audio/<slug>.instr.mp3     the built soundtrack\n  public/content/<slug>.script.json the picture timeline, frame-accurate\n\"\"\"\nimport json, io, os, subprocess, sys\n\nBASE = os.path.dirname(os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__)))\nTL = json.load(io.open(os.path.join(BASE, \"public/content/timeline.json\"), encoding=\"utf-8\"))\nAUD = os.path.join(BASE, \"public/audio\")\nGEN = os.path.join(BASE, \"recall/generated\")\nTMP = os.path.join(BASE, \"align\", \"_instr\")\nos.makedirs(TMP, exist_ok=True)\n\n# How long each introduction card holds, in seconds.\nHOLD = {\"read\": 5.0, \"tell\": 5.4, \"colour\": 5.6, \"recall\": 6.2}\n# Beat around the Space press.\nPRESS_LEAD, PRESS_TAIL = 1.1, 1.4\n\nALOUD = \"aecho=0.86:0.9:22:0.10\"\n\n\ndef run(cmd):\n    r = subprocess.run(cmd, shell=True, capture_output=True, text=True)\n    if r.returncode != 0:\n        print(r.stderr[-1200:]); sys.exit(\"FAILED\")\n\n\ndef build(slug, introduce):\n    \"\"\"introduce: which step cards to play. Q1 gets all; later ones get none.\"\"\"\n    segs = TL[slug][\"segments\"]\n    src = os.path.join(AUD, f\"{slug}.mp3\")\n    recall = os.path.join(GEN, f\"{slug}.recall.wav\")\n\n    pieces, beats, t = [], [], 0.0\n\n    def silence(dur, kind, **extra):\n        nonlocal t\n        p = os.path.join(TMP, f\"{slug}_{len(pieces):03d}.wav\")\n        run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -f lavfi -i anullsrc=r=48000:cl=mono '\n            f'-t {dur:.3f} \"{p}\"')\n        pieces.append(p)\n        beats.append({\"kind\": kind, \"start\": round(t, 3), \"end\": round(t + dur, 3), **extra})\n        t += dur\n\n    def take(a, b, kind, **extra):\n        nonlocal t\n        p = os.path.join(TMP, f\"{slug}_{len(pieces):03d}.wav\")\n        run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -ss {a:.3f} -to {b:.3f} -i \"{src}\" '\n            f'-af \"{ALOUD}\" -ar 48000 -ac 1 \"{p}\"')\n        pieces.append(p)\n        beats.append({\"kind\": kind, \"start\": round(t, 3), \"end\": round(t + (b - a), 3), **extra})\n        t += b - a\n\n    for i, s in enumerate(segs):\n        gi, ns = s[\"gi\"], s[\"chunkNs\"]\n\n        if i == 0 and \"read\" in introduce:\n            silence(HOLD[\"read\"], \"card\", step=\"read\")\n        # Step 1. Silent, and exactly as long as he really took.\n        silence(s[\"readEnd\"] - s[\"readStart\"], \"read\", gi=gi, chunkNs=ns)\n\n        if i == 0 and \"tell\" in introduce:\n            silence(HOLD[\"tell\"], \"card\", step=\"tell\")\n        # The release, then everything he says out loud, in one take.\n        if s[\"saidStart\"] > s[\"readEnd\"]:\n            take(s[\"readEnd\"], s[\"saidStart\"], \"release\", gi=gi, chunkNs=ns)\n\n        if i == 0 and \"colour\" in introduce:\n            silence(HOLD[\"colour\"], \"card\", step=\"colour\")\n        take(s[\"saidStart\"], s[\"workEnd\"], \"speak\", gi=gi, chunkNs=ns)\n\n    # The Recall: introduced, then the press, then he gives it back.\n    if \"recall\" in introduce:\n        silence(HOLD[\"recall\"], \"card\", step=\"recall\")\n    silence(PRESS_LEAD, \"press\", phase=\"before\")\n    rdur = float(subprocess.run(\n        f'ffprobe -v error -show_entries format=duration -of csv=p=0 \"{recall}\"',\n        shell=True, capture_output=True, text=True).stdout.strip())\n    p = os.path.join(TMP, f\"{slug}_{len(pieces):03d}.wav\")\n    run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -i \"{recall}\" -af \"{ALOUD}\" -ar 48000 -ac 1 \"{p}\"')\n    pieces.append(p)\n    beats.append({\"kind\": \"recall\", \"start\": round(t, 3), \"end\": round(t + rdur, 3)})\n    t += rdur\n    silence(PRESS_TAIL, \"settle\")\n\n    lst = os.path.join(TMP, f\"{slug}.txt\")\n    with io.open(lst, \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as fh:\n        for p in pieces:\n            fh.write(f\"file '{os.path.basename(p)}'\\n\")\n    voice = os.path.join(TMP, f\"{slug}_voice.wav\")\n    run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -f concat -safe 0 -i \"{lst}\" -c copy \"{voice}\"')\n\n    # The bed, gated over the silent reading only, so it carries those stretches.\n    gate = []\n    for b in beats:\n        if b[\"kind\"] == \"read\" and b[\"end\"] - b[\"start\"] > 0.6:\n            gate.append(f\"between(t,{b['start']:.3f},{b['end']:.3f})*\"\n                        f\"min(1,min((t-{b['start']:.3f})/0.55,({b['end']:.3f}-t)/0.5))\")\n    env = \"+\".join(gate) if gate else \"0\"\n    bed = os.path.join(TMP, f\"{slug}_bed.wav\")\n    run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -f lavfi -i \"sine=frequency=98:duration={t:.3f}:sample_rate=48000\" '\n        f'-f lavfi -i \"sine=frequency=147:duration={t:.3f}:sample_rate=48000\" '\n        f'-filter_complex \"[0:a]volume=0.075[a];[1:a]volume=0.030[b];[a][b]amix=inputs=2:normalize=0,'\n        f'lowpass=f=420,volume=\\'{env}\\':eval=frame[o]\" -map \"[o]\" -ar 48000 -ac 1 \"{bed}\"')\n\n    out = os.path.join(AUD, f\"{slug}.instr.mp3\")\n    run(f'ffmpeg -y -v error -i \"{voice}\" -i \"{bed}\" '\n        f'-filter_complex \"[0:a][1:a]amix=inputs=2:normalize=0:duration=first[o]\" '\n        f'-map \"[o]\" -codec:a libmp3lame -b:a 192k \"{out}\"')\n\n    script = {\"slug\": slug, \"duration\": round(t, 3), \"beats\": beats}\n    json.dump(script, io.open(os.path.join(BASE, f\"public/content/{slug}.script.json\"),\n                              \"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\"), indent=1)\n\n    got = float(subprocess.run(\n        f'ffprobe -v error -show_entries format=duration -of csv=p=0 \"{out}\"',\n        shell=True, capture_output=True, text=True).stdout.strip())\n    kinds = {}\n    for b in beats:\n        kinds[b[\"kind\"]] = kinds.get(b[\"kind\"], 0) + 1\n    print(f\"{slug}: {len(beats)} beats {kinds}\")\n    print(f\"   script {t:.2f}s | audio {got:.2f}s | \"\n          f\"{'OK' if abs(got - t) < 0.15 else '*** DRIFT ***'} | \"\n          f\"was {TL[slug]['duration']:.2f}s, +{t - TL[slug]['duration']:.1f}s of teaching\")\n\n\n# Q1 introduces every step. Q2 and Q3 are already understood, so they only\n# introduce nothing at all: the labels alone carry them (R5-Q1).\nbuild(\"PT105_S2_Q1\", {\"read\", \"tell\", \"colour\", \"recall\"})\nfor s in (\"PT105_S2_Q2\", \"PT105_S2_Q3\"):\n    build(s, set())\n"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\video\\src\\pov\\Instruction.tsx", "content": "import React from \"react\";\nimport { interpolate, Easing } from \"remotion\";\nimport { SEC } from \"../rrc/tokens\";\nimport { Plate3D } from \"./Float\";\n\n/*\n * The teaching stops.\n *\n * Each step is introduced ONCE, the first time it happens, with the picture\n * held and his audio paused. After that a small label carries it (R5-Q1).\n *\n * The step names are the app's own student-facing words (R5-Q3), so a viewer\n * who opens the drill straight after meets one vocabulary rather than two. The\n * footer visible on the monitor in these very shots reads \"Read it. Tell it back\n * and colour it in as you go\", and these cards agree with it.\n *\n * Copy rules that apply here: no em dashes, and the word the app grades on is\n * never spoken to the student (R4-Q8), so the cards say what he carries in plain\n * language instead.\n */\n\nexport type StepKey = \"read\" | \"tell\" | \"colour\" | \"recall\";\n\nexport const STEPS: Record<StepKey, { n: number; name: string; says: string }> = {\n  read: {\n    n: 1,\n    name: \"Reading\",\n    says: \"He reads the chunk in his head. Nothing is said out loud. Watch how long he takes.\",\n  },\n  tell: {\n    n: 2,\n    name: \"Tell it back\",\n    says: \"He looks away from the screen and says that chunk back in his own words.\",\n  },\n  colour: {\n    n: 3,\n    name: \"Colouring In\",\n    says: \"Now he reacts to it. What it reminds him of, what he doubts, what he thinks it means.\",\n  },\n  recall: {\n    n: 4,\n    name: \"The Recall\",\n    says: \"He presses Space. The stimulus disappears. He gives the whole argument back from memory.\",\n  },\n};\n\n/** Named while it is happening, once the step has been introduced. */\nexport const StepLabel: React.FC<{ step: StepKey; enter: number }> = ({ step, enter }) => {\n  const s = STEPS[step];\n  return (\n    <Plate3D x={132} y={106} w={318} tiltX={5} tiltY={7} rgb=\"150,170,255\" enter={enter}>\n      <div style={{ display: \"flex\", alignItems: \"baseline\", gap: 11 }}>\n        <span style={{ fontSize: 15, letterSpacing: 1.6, color: \"rgba(190,205,255,0.85)\" }}>\n          STEP {s.n}\n        </span>\n        <span style={{ fontSize: 30, fontWeight: 500, letterSpacing: -0.3 }}>{s.name}</span>\n      </div>\n    </Plate3D>\n  );\n};\n\n/** The full stop. Picture holds, his audio is silent, this is all there is. */\nexport const StepCard: React.FC<{ step: StepKey; k: number }> = ({ step, k }) => {\n  const s = STEPS[step];\n  const enter = interpolate(k, [0, 0.16], [0, 1], { extrapolateRight: \"clamp\", easing: Easing.out(Easing.cubic) });\n  const leave = interpolate(k, [0.88, 1], [1, 0], { extrapolateLeft: \"clamp\", easing: Easing.in(Easing.cubic) });\n  const on = Math.min(enter, leave);\n  return (\n    <>\n      {/* The room quietens so the instruction is unmistakably not part of it. */}\n      <div\n        style={{\n          position: \"absolute\", inset: 0, zIndex: 3,\n          background: \"rgba(6,7,14,0.62)\", opacity: on,\n        }}\n      />\n      <div style={{ position: \"absolute\", inset: 0, zIndex: 5, display: \"flex\",\n                    alignItems: \"center\", justifyContent: \"center\" }}>\n        <Plate3D x={0} y={0} w={880} tiltX={4} tiltY={-5} rgb=\"150,170,255\" enter={on}>\n          <div style={{ padding: \"14px 16px\" }}>\n            <div style={{ display: \"flex\", alignItems: \"baseline\", gap: 15, marginBottom: 20 }}>\n              <span\n                style={{\n                  fontSize: 19, letterSpacing: 2.4, color: \"#bcccff\",\n                  padding: \"6px 13px\", borderRadius: 7,\n                  background: \"rgba(30,40,86,0.75)\",\n                }}\n              >\n                STEP {s.n}\n              </span>\n              <span style={{ fontFamily: SEC.serif, fontSize: 62, fontWeight: 600, letterSpacing: -1 }}>\n                {s.name}\n              </span>\n            </div>\n            <div style={{ fontSize: 34, lineHeight: 1.45, color: \"rgba(255,255,255,0.92)\" }}>\n              {s.says}\n            </div>\n          </div>\n        </Plate3D>\n      </div>\n    </>\n  );\n};\n"}

{"file_path": "C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os\\projects\\video\\src\\pov\\POVInstructional.tsx", "content": "import React from \"react\";\nimport { AbsoluteFill, Audio, staticFile, useCurrentFrame, useVideoConfig, interpolate, Easing } from \"remotion\";\nimport { Screen } from \"../rrc/Screen\";\nimport type { Chunk } from \"../rrc/Stimulus\";\nimport { Room, Monitor, POVStimulus } from \"./Room\";\nimport { SpeechBubble, enterAt } from \"./Float\";\nimport { StepCard, StepLabel, type StepKey } from \"./Instruction\";\nimport type { Seg, Phase } from \"./beats\";\n\nexport type Cue = { start: number; end: number; text: string };\n\nexport type Beat = {\n  kind: \"card\" | \"read\" | \"release\" | \"speak\" | \"press\" | \"recall\" | \"settle\";\n  start: number;\n  end: number;\n  step?: StepKey;\n  gi?: number;\n  chunkNs?: number[];\n  phase?: string;\n};\n\nexport type Script = { slug: string; duration: number; beats: Beat[] };\n\nconst mmss = (t: number) =>\n  `${Math.floor(t / 60)}:${String(Math.floor(t % 60)).padStart(2, \"0\")}`;\n\n/*\n * The instructional cut.\n *\n * Driven entirely by the beat script that align/build_script.py emitted\n * alongside the soundtrack, so picture and audio cannot drift.\n *\n *   card     the picture holds and a step is introduced. His audio is silent.\n *   read     SILENT. The eye tracks the chunk, everything else is soft.\n *   release  focus lets go before the eye does.\n *   speak    he has glanced off; what he says arrives beside him.\n *   press    the app's own Space chip depresses.\n *   recall   the real drill exactly: paper stays, text gone.\n */\nexport const POVInstructional: React.FC<{\n  slug: string;\n  chunks: Chunk[];\n  script: Script;\n  cues: Cue[];\n  cueOffsets: Record<string, number>;\n  audioSrc: string;\n  where: string;\n  qIndex: number;\n  qTotal: number;\n}> = ({ chunks, script, cues, cueOffsets, audioSrc, where, qIndex, qTotal }) => {\n  const frame = useCurrentFrame();\n  const { fps, width, height, durationInFrames } = useVideoConfig();\n  const t = frame / fps;\n\n  const beat = script.beats.find((b) => t >= b.start && t < b.end) ?? script.beats[script.beats.length - 1];\n  const k = beat.end > beat.start ? (t - beat.start) / (beat.end - beat.start) : 1;\n\n  // Which step is on screen. Cards name themselves; the rest map from the beat.\n  const stepOf: Partial<Record<Beat[\"kind\"], StepKey>> = {\n    read: \"read\", release: \"tell\", speak: \"colour\", press: \"recall\", recall: \"recall\",\n  };\n  const step: StepKey | null = beat.kind === \"card\" ? beat.step! : (stepOf[beat.kind] ?? null);\n\n  // A step is only labelled once its card has played.\n  const introduced = new Set(\n    script.beats.filter((b) => b.kind === \"card\" && b.start < t).map((b) => b.step!),\n  );\n  const anyCards = script.beats.some((b) => b.kind === \"card\");\n  const labelOn = step !== null && beat.kind !== \"card\" && (!anyCards || introduced.has(step));\n\n  // Frames since this beat began, for springing the label in.\n  const beatFrames = Math.round((t - beat.start) * fps);\n\n  // The stimulus is gone from the press onward, exactly as the drill does it.\n  const recalling = beat.kind === \"recall\" || beat.kind === \"settle\" ||\n    (beat.kind === \"press\" && k > 0.55);\n  const pressed = beat.kind === \"press\"\n    ? interpolate(k, [0.5, 0.58, 0.72, 0.8], [0, 1, 1, 0], { extrapolateLeft: \"clamp\", extrapolateRight: \"clamp\" })\n    : 0;\n\n  // Cue lookup runs in ORIGINAL recording time, because the cues were written\n  // against his real run. cueOffsets maps each speak beat back onto it.\n  const off = beat.gi !== undefined ? cueOffsets[String(beat.gi)] : undefined;\n  const srcT = off !== undefined ? t - off : -1;\n  const cueIdx = srcT >= 0 ? cues.findIndex((c) => srcT >= c.start && srcT < c.end) : -1;\n  const cue = cueIdx >= 0 ? cues[cueIdx] : null;\n  const showBubble = (beat.kind === \"speak\" || beat.kind === \"release\") && !!cue;\n\n  // A synthetic segment so the existing focus logic keeps working unchanged.\n  const activeNs = beat.chunkNs ?? [];\n  const phase: Phase =\n    beat.kind === \"read\" ? \"read\" :\n    beat.kind === \"release\" ? \"release\" :\n    beat.kind === \"speak\" ? \"speak\" : \"confirm\";\n  const seg: Seg = {\n    gi: beat.gi ?? 0, chunkNs: activeNs,\n    readStart: beat.start, readEnd: beat.kind === \"read\" ? beat.end : beat.start,\n    saidStart: beat.kind === \"read\" ? beat.end : beat.start,\n    workEnd: beat.end, readCoverage: 1,\n  };\n\n  const through = Math.min(1, frame / durationInFrames);\n\n  return (\n    <AbsoluteFill>\n      <Audio src={staticFile(audioSrc)} />\n\n      <Room seg={seg} t={t} frameW={width} frameH={height}>\n        <Monitor frameW={width} frameH={height}>\n          <Screen\n            through={through}\n            clock={mmss(t)}\n            where={`${where}  ·  ${qIndex} of ${qTotal}`}\n            instruction={recalling\n              ? \"Say the whole argument back from memory.\"\n              : \"Read it. Tell it back and colour it in as you go.\"}\n            keyDoes={recalling ? \"next stimulus\" : \"recall full stimulus\"}\n            pressed={pressed}\n          >\n            {recalling ? null : (\n              <POVStimulus chunks={chunks} activeNs={activeNs} phase={phase} k={k} />\n            )}\n          </Screen>\n        </Monitor>\n\n        {showBubble && cue && (\n          <SpeechBubble\n            key={cueIdx}\n            text={cue.text}\n            enter={enterAt(Math.round((srcT - cue.start) * fps), fps)}\n            x={1120}\n            y={392}\n          />\n        )}\n\n        {labelOn && step && (\n          <StepLabel step={step} enter={enterAt(beatFrames, fps)} />\n        )}\n      </Room>\n\n      {beat.kind === \"card\" && beat.step && <StepCard step={beat.step} k={k} />}\n    </AbsoluteFill>\n  );\n};\n"}

Picture is cut to the SCRIPT, not the mp3.\n\n### Reading is now genuinely silent\nHis voice is muted across every read span and the span keeps its real duration.\nThe held-tone bed carries those stretches alone, so its level was raised\n(0.075 / 0.030 from 0.055 / 0.022) with 0.55s/0.5s fades.\n\n### THREE FRAMING BUGS, all the same root cause\nEverything inside the camera wrapper is scaled by the zoom, so anchors are in\nROOM space, not frame space: `frameX = 960 + (roomX - 960) * zoom`.\n1. The instructional cut is rendering. While it finishes, here's what changed and why.

**Your six notes, all built:**

Steps are now **named and introduced**. Each one gets a full stop the first time it happens — the picture holds, your audio pauses, and a card says what's about to occur. After that a small label carries it. Q1 gains 46 seconds of teaching, Q2 gains 32, Q3 gains 24.

**Reading is genuinely silent now.** Not filtered, not ducked — muted. You were right that it read as speaking aloud, and the reason is that filtering can't turn projected speech into thought. The read spans keep their real duration though, so the viewer watches the eye take exactly the time you took. The held tone carries those stretches alone.

**The Recall is built.** Space depresses the app's *own* key chip in the footer, the stimulus clears, the footer switches to "Say the whole argument back from memory", and your cloned voice gives it back. Exactly the drill, nothing added, as you asked.

**One decision I made against your wording.** You said "Step 2: Rephrase". I used **"Tell it back"** instead, because that's the app's own student-facing label — and the footer visible on the monitor *in the same shot* already says "Read it. Tell it back and colour it in as you go." Two words for one thing, ninety seconds apart, is the confusion your vocabulary rules exist to prevent. Say the word and I'll switch it.

**Three framing bugs, one root cause.** Everything inside the camera wrapper scales with the zoom, so anchors are in room space, not frame space. The 1.30 zoom I set earlier to hide the plate edges was **cropping the monitor itself** — the app's own footer instruction was being cut off the left of frame. For an instructional film, cropping the thing being read is the wrong trade. Zoom is now 1.12 with a smaller glance, and the whole monitor sits in frame.

## user

Base directory for this skill: C:\Users\Joshu\.claude\skills\session-handoff

# Session Handoff

Produce a repeatable end-of-session summary so Joshua can `/clear` and start a fresh agent without losing continuity. The next agent should pick up by reading this summary alone.

This is a **context-handoff artifact**, not a status report. The audience is a future instance of you, not a stakeholder.

**Handoff vs. wrapup:** run `session-handoff` when the *context window* is the problem — nearing ~250k tokens (the dumb zone), or passing work between Claude and Codex. It is chat-only and writes nothing. Run `wrapup` when the *work day* is done — it strikes tasks, refreshes `wiki/hot.md`, and appends `wiki/log.md`. They can be run back to back (wrapup to persist, then handoff to reset).

## When to invoke

Joshua says: "session handoff", "hand off", "handoff summary", "summarize before I clear", or any near-equivalent. Note "let's wrap up" belongs to `wrapup`, not here — if he says that and the context window is also deep, run `wrapup` first, then offer this. Also invoke proactively if he says he's about to `/clear` without having run it, or if the session is clearly deep into the dumb zone.

## How to produce the summary

1. **Review the full conversation**, not just the last few turns. Handoffs miss things when they only summarize recent context.
2. **Pull state from these sources (in order):**
   - Plan files referenced this session (check `~/.claude/plans/` if a plan was mentioned).
   - TodoWrite state — any in-progress or pending tasks.
   - Background processes started with `run_in_background` — shell IDs are load-bearing for the next agent.
   - Files created or modified this session — you know what you touched; don't grep to re-discover.
   - Memory files written or updated (`~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/`).
   - Unresolved questions — things you asked Joshua that never got a clear answer, or things he asked that got deflected.
3. **Do NOT audit the filesystem.** This is synthesis of what happened in THIS session. No `git log`, no broad `Glob` sweeps. If you didn't touch it this session, it doesn't belong here.
4. **Produce the output in chat.** Do not write a file. Do not update memory. Chat-only.

## Output template — use exactly this structure, every time

```
# Session Handoff — <one-line title of what this session was about>

## Where it started
<2-3 sentences: what Joshua asked for, key framing or constraints that emerged>

## Decisions locked + what shipped
- <decision or change> — <why, and where it lives (absolute path if a file)>
- ...

## Key files for next session
- `<absolute path>` — <why the next agent should read this first>
- Plan file: `<path>` (if a plan drove the session)
- Memory files touched: `<paths>` (if any)

## Running state
- Background processes: <shell IDs + what they are + how to kill> — or "none"
- Dev servers / ports: <url + port> — or "none"
- Open worktrees / branches: <paths> — or "none"

## Verified state as of <HH:MM>
- <claim about live/system state> — <how it was checked, and when>
- <claim> — NOT re-verified this session; last confirmed <when>
- ...

## Verification — how to confirm things still work
- `<command>` — <expected outcome>
- ...

## Deferred + open questions
- Deferred: <item> — <why pushed to later>
- OPEN (must be resolved or re-asked before new work): <question needing Joshua's input> — <context>

## Pick up here
<1-2 sentences: the single most likely next action for a fresh agent>
```

## Hard rules

1. **Chat output only.** Never write the handoff to a file. Never update memory from this skill. (That is `wrapup`'s job, not this one.)
2. **Never invent state.** If a section has nothing to report, write "none" — do not omit the section. Structure stability is the whole point.
3. **Absolute paths always.** The next agent may have a different working directory, or be Codex.
4. **If a plan file drove the session, name it first** in "Key files" so the next agent reads it before anything else.
5. **No emojis, no hype, no "great job" summaries.** Terse and concrete — paths, commands, shell IDs, decisions. Tone of a seasoned engineer handing off at end-of-shift.
6. **Background process IDs are critical.** If you started any `run_in_background` shells, their IDs must appear in "Running state" with the kill command — the next agent cannot find them otherwise.
7. **Stamp verified state, and separate it from assumed state.** Run `date "+%H:%M"` and use the real time. Every claim about live systems (deploy status, DB contents, what was sent, what is published) is either something you actually checked this session — say how — or explicitly marked NOT re-verified. A handoff that presents stale belief as current state makes the next session re-derive everything, which is the exact cost this skill exists to remove.
8. **Open questions are obligations, not notes.** Anything under OPEN must be resolved by the next session or re-asked to Joshua before it starts new work. Requirements have been silently re-typed across sessions, and a promised artifact once never arrived because nobody re-asked. If a question was open in the handoff you are picking up from, it is the first thing you raise.

## Anti-patterns — do not do these

- Summarizing the last 3 turns and calling it a handoff.
- Listing files by relative path.
- Skipping "Running state" because "nothing is running" — write "none" instead.
- Writing the summary to any file. This is chat-only by design.
- Adding a "what went well / what went poorly" retrospective. This isn't a retro.
- Recommending next steps beyond the single "Pick up here" line. The next agent decides; you just hand off.
